Later work that needs per-request session state (a role per caller, row-level-security settings) must use `SET LOCAL` inside the call's transaction or a pool per role, never a session-level `SET`. *`SET` state leaking between pooled sessions:* only `statement_timeout` is ever set; direct mode tracks it per connection and `RESET`s it for "server default" (test `time_limits_follow_the_command_class_and_never_leak_between_callers`: mixed classes on one connection each see exactly their own limit, also in a transaction and a snapshot of a class without one); transaction mode sets nothing on a session at all. *Locks that exclude nobody:* a pooler in transaction mode with the locks taken through it; the start-up refuses the configuration, and refuses a lock address that reaches another database; what it cannot see is a lock address that is another transaction-mode pooler in front of the same database (documented). *Dead or stale connections:* checked on checkout when idle, dropped with their neighbours on failure, never put back after an error that leaves their state unknown; a write is not repeated. *Disclosure:* the 503 bodies are fixed words and a hint; the driver's text goes to the error string (now with SQLSTATE and the server's message, no detail); configuration errors never echo a value that looks like an address; `/metrics` carries counts and the three pool names, no record, user or path; the lock URL is validated with the same rules as the database URL and its text is never echoed. *Out of scope:* a compromised database or pooler; a network that drops packets silently (keep-alive and a user timeout are set where the platform supports them); admission control above the pool.

**Acceptance tests (named).** Pool without a database (`crates/kernel/obop-storage/src/pool_tests.rs`): `connections_are_opened_on_demand_up_to_the_maximum_and_reused`; `exhaustion_answers_busy_after_the_acquire_timeout_and_notes_the_fault`; `the_wait_queue_is_bounded_and_the_surplus_is_refused_at_once`; `a_connection_that_comes_back_wakes_a_waiter`; `many_threads_share_a_small_pool_and_never_exceed_it`; `a_broken_connection_and_a_panicking_holder_are_never_put_back`; `idle_connections_are_closed_after_the_idle_timeout`; `a_connection_past_its_lifetime_is_closed_instead_of_reused`; `the_health_check_replaces_a_dead_idle_connection_on_checkout`; `a_recently_used_connection_is_not_pinged`; `an_unreachable_database_is_unavailable_before_the_timeout_and_the_pool_recovers`; `a_slow_connect_does_not_hold_the_caller_past_its_timeout` (and the connection that arrives late serves the next caller, no other is made); `a_panic_while_connecting_gives_the_slot_back`; `a_waiter_tries_again_when_the_pause_after_a_failed_connect_is_over`; `a_seeded_connection_is_checked_like_any_other_that_sat_idle`; `a_hanging_ping_holds_the_caller_no_longer_than_its_time_allows`; `long_reads_are_capped_and_leave_room_for_interactive_callers`; `purge_idle_closes_every_idle_connection_and_frees_the_room`; `a_seeded_connection_serves_first`; `pools_of_the_same_name_add_up_in_the_report`; `a_dropped_pool_leaves_the_report_and_lets_go_of_its_manager`. The fault rule: `fault::tests::{messages_start_with_the_code_word_and_are_recognised_again, the_first_fault_wins_and_take_clears_it, a_note_belongs_to_its_thread, a_request_scope_blocks_writes_after_a_fault_and_remembers_commits}`; `testing::tests::{after_a_refused_read_the_guard_refuses_changes_in_a_request_and_not_outside_one, commits_are_noted_except_counters_values_dedup_markers_and_the_security_log}`; the layer over a store that refuses calls (`obop-http` `ops::tests`): `a_swallowed_refusal_before_any_change_is_answered_503_with_retry_after`, `a_request_that_committed_a_change_keeps_its_answer`, `after_a_refused_read_the_request_writes_nothing_and_says_503`, `the_503_speaks_the_shape_of_its_route_and_keeps_its_headers`, `the_health_routes_decide_for_themselves`, `a_saturated_pool_is_busy_not_down`; the vault (`shared::tests::a_refused_read_of_the_vault_fails_the_call_and_leaves_the_stored_vault_alone`); a create or update whose read-back is refused (`engine_tests::a_change_that_committed_is_answered_even_when_its_read_back_is_refused`, on the guarded memory backend); the OAuth refresh (`obop-oauth` `tests::a_refused_lookup_never_ends_a_grant_and_a_refused_follow_up_never_loses_the_new_tokens`); the security log (`audit::tests::an_event_is_not_lost_to_a_store_that_is_busy_for_a_moment`); connectors (`model::tests::a_policy_that_cannot_be_read_closes_the_gates_instead_of_opening_them`, `connectors` module `a_store_that_refuses_a_lookup_does_not_dead_letter_a_queued_delivery`); the control plane (`obop` `the_control_plane_answers_503_when_the_store_is_busy_not_401_or_404`, `obop-platform` `a_busy_store_does_not_leave_an_instance_deleting_for_ever`). **The partial rule and what follows from it:** `fault::tests` (a needed step refused after a commit is partial and one before any commit is not; compensations undo a counted change; `patiently` does not wait for the guard's own refusal); `testing::tests::bookkeeping_is_allowed_after_a_refusal_and_the_log_and_dedup_markers_are_not_changes`; blobs (`blobs::tests::a_blob_hold_refused_for_a_moment_is_tried_again_and_a_lasting_refusal_is_a_partial_503`); the shop (`obop-site` `a_checkout_refused_by_a_busy_store_releases_its_key_and_the_resubmit_makes_the_order`); webhooks (`obop-payments` `a_completion_met_by_a_busy_store_is_retried_not_swallowed_and_not_taken_for_a_duplicate`, `connectors` `an_inbound_webhook_met_by_a_busy_store_is_not_swallowed_and_its_retry_is_applied`, `obop-connectors` `webhook::tests::a_store_that_refuses_is_neither_a_new_event_nor_a_replay_and_a_claim_can_be_given_back`); accounts (`obop-auth` `admin_tests::{offboarding_with_a_token_list_that_is_refused_fails_as_partial_and_can_be_repeated, offboarding_goes_on_when_the_store_refuses_for_a_moment, a_password_reset_whose_sessions_cannot_be_listed_is_a_partial_failure, a_lookup_that_is_refused_is_the_stores_trouble_and_not_a_400_or_a_404}`); the vault and the MCP session (`shared::tests::{a_vault_save_refused_for_a_moment_is_tried_again, a_vault_save_that_stays_refused_after_a_commit_is_a_partial_failure}`, `mcp::tests::{a_session_save_refused_for_a_moment_is_tried_again, a_session_save_that_stays_refused_after_a_commit_is_a_partial_failure}`); handler errors (`obop-http` `fault_answers::*`); the connector worker (`connectors` `a_retention_run_that_cannot_read_what_it_decides_on_deletes_nothing_and_is_repeated_soon`); OAuth (`obop-oauth` `tests::{a_rotation_the_store_refuses_leaves_no_live_access_token_behind_and_says_when_to_retry, a_code_exchange_the_store_refuses_gives_the_code_back_and_leaves_nothing_behind, ending_a_grant_is_never_reported_when_the_store_refused_it}`); the documents (`openapi::tests::every_operation_declares_the_503_with_its_retry_after_and_applied_marker`); the batch locks (`pg_pool_tests::{a_batch_takes_at_most_the_stripe_count_of_advisory_locks_whatever_its_size, the_locks_of_a_thousand_row_batch_in_the_server_are_the_stripes_not_the_records, thousand_row_batches_from_many_threads_do_not_fill_the_servers_lock_table, large_batches_that_name_the_same_records_in_opposite_order_do_not_deadlock}`, the second of which asks the server how many advisory locks the transaction holds); two uploads of one file (`blob::tests::the_same_bytes_uploaded_twice_at_once_are_stored_once_and_both_uploads_succeed`, which fails with a duplicate key without the lock); the address rule (`crates/obop/tests/pool.rs` `a_command_given_its_own_direct_address_is_not_refused_in_a_transaction_pooler_layout`, which also runs `obop --db URL serve` and `obop --dev-auth serve --db URL`; `ops::tests::every_flag_that_names_a_database_is_an_address_of_the_commands_own`). **Third round:** money and stock (`obop-payments` `a_refused_read_of_the_order_after_a_payment_is_never_taken_for_a_gone_order_and_never_refunds`, `a_late_payment_for_a_cancelled_order_is_refunded_once_even_when_the_store_refuses_a_call_on_the_way`, `a_cancel_whose_units_cannot_be_given_back_is_a_partial_503_and_the_sweep_finishes_it`, `a_refund_is_not_made_for_an_order_whose_payment_cannot_be_looked_up`, `the_sweep_never_cancels_an_order_whose_payment_it_could_not_look_up`; `obop-site` `a_claim_with_no_order_behind_it_is_taken_over_when_old_and_respected_while_young`, `a_claim_the_store_refused_is_not_a_claim_and_releases_nothing_of_the_submit_that_owns_the_key`; `obop-payments` `an_order_that_cannot_be_marked_paid_for_another_reason_is_never_answered_as_done`, `a_cancelled_order_that_never_reserved_units_returns_none_and_the_sweep_leaves_it_alone`, `a_cancel_of_a_pending_order_whose_payment_already_succeeded_returns_the_money`, `a_refund_whose_money_went_back_but_whose_record_was_refused_is_a_partial_503_that_says_so`, `a_refund_whose_units_could_not_go_back_is_finished_by_the_sweep_and_a_delivered_one_owes_none`, and the fifth round's `moving_an_order_with_an_update_returns_the_money_and_the_units_like_the_action`, `an_approved_plan_that_moves_the_state_runs_the_same_wrappers`, `the_sweep_returns_the_money_of_a_cancelled_order_whose_payment_succeeded`, `a_cancel_whose_refund_record_is_refused_never_leaves_an_order_the_sweep_can_mark_paid` and `a_refund_of_delivered_goods_never_leaves_units_owed_whichever_step_the_store_refuses` (each of the last two refuses the store from every call position in turn, for longer than a step is tried again), `a_refused_call_of_the_sweep_does_not_refuse_the_buyers_own_checkout`; `obop-site` `tests::{a_key_given_back_between_a_failed_claim_and_the_read_of_it_is_claimed_again, a_release_whose_own_read_is_refused_deletes_nothing_it_cannot_prove_is_its_own}`; `fault::tests::a_detached_part_keeps_its_faults_and_changes_to_itself`); `/mcp` and approvals through the real application (`obop` `mcp_tests::a_tool_call_the_store_refused_is_a_503_that_says_what_was_applied_never_a_200_tool_error`, `a_batch_of_tool_calls_that_met_a_refusal_after_a_commit_is_a_partial_503_too`, `an_approval_whose_plan_cannot_be_put_back_says_the_proposal_is_lost_and_logs_it`, `invoicing` `an_approval_refused_before_it_changed_anything_can_be_approved_again` (every position of a bundled `invoice.post` run), `an_approval_the_store_refused_before_it_changed_anything_can_be_repeated`, `every_generated_operation_of_a_real_instance_document_declares_the_503`); the repeat that finishes the work (`obop-auth` `admin_tests::{a_group_change_that_could_not_relimit_the_sessions_is_finished_by_repeating_it, turning_two_factor_off_that_could_not_end_the_other_sessions_is_finished_by_repeating_it, an_administrators_two_factor_reset_that_could_not_end_the_sessions_is_finished_by_repeating_it}`, `passkey_tests::{removing_a_passkey_that_could_not_end_the_other_sessions_is_finished_by_repeating_it, removing_a_passkey_with_a_refusal_at_any_call_never_panics_and_the_repeat_leaves_nothing_behind, an_administrators_reset_never_answers_done_while_the_passkey_list_cannot_be_read}`, `admin_tests::{a_disable_or_an_offboarding_that_could_not_end_the_tokens_does_not_bring_them_back_on_enable, a_password_change_that_could_not_end_the_other_sessions_says_what_the_person_can_do, a_note_for_the_repeat_is_not_a_change_so_a_refused_change_is_never_answered_partial, offboarding_goes_on_when_the_store_refuses_for_a_moment}`, and the fifth round's `admin_tests::{a_note_that_is_kept_or_taken_back_leaves_the_notes_of_the_other_changes_alone, a_pending_offboarding_survives_a_later_reset_so_enabling_still_finishes_it, a_note_is_kept_when_the_outcome_of_the_change_is_unknown_so_the_repeat_still_ends_the_sessions, a_reset_that_was_refused_before_it_removed_anything_leaves_no_note_behind, a_second_step_of_the_sign_in_gives_no_session_to_an_account_disabled_in_between, a_password_change_whose_limit_could_not_be_lifted_says_to_sign_in_again_not_to_sign_out_everywhere}` and `passkey_tests::{a_reset_that_stopped_after_the_passkeys_went_is_finished_by_repeating_it_recovery_codes_too, a_passkey_second_step_gives_no_session_to_an_account_disabled_in_between, a_passkey_enrolment_whose_limit_could_not_be_lifted_is_never_answered_as_done}`; the sixth round's `admin_tests::{a_repeated_offboarding_refused_at_its_own_write_leaves_the_note_of_the_first_one, a_note_that_existed_is_put_back_and_not_removed_when_the_repeat_of_its_change_is_refused, a_two_factor_enrolment_whose_limit_could_not_be_lifted_is_never_answered_as_done, a_password_change_never_frees_a_session_that_owes_enrolment_because_a_policy_read_was_refused, a_session_made_while_its_account_is_being_disabled_is_revoked_and_not_left_to_wake_up, a_lockout_counter_the_store_would_not_give_is_a_lockout_and_not_no_attempts}`); the security log (`audit::tests::an_event_after_a_refused_call_of_the_same_request_is_also_tried_four_times`, `audit::tests::an_entry_that_stays_refused_is_reported_to_its_caller_which_may_be_its_only_trace`); an approved plan that is put back or not (`obop-commands` `tests::an_approved_plan_comes_back_when_nothing_was_applied_and_stays_gone_when_the_outcome_is_unknown`); an outbound flow that leaves only with its trace (`connectors` `tests::records_leave_only_with_a_trace_and_a_delivery_is_never_answered_as_nothing_applied`, which refuses the store from every call of a manual send in turn, once and for good, and `tests::an_event_met_by_one_refused_call_is_delivered_once_not_lost_and_not_twice`); a connection that died under a deadlocked transaction (`pg_pool_tests::a_connection_that_died_under_a_deadlocked_transaction_is_not_given_to_its_rerun`); the sixth round: connectors (`egress::tests::{a_mask_refused_between_its_two_writes_never_sends_a_token_that_cannot_be_restored, a_refused_read_of_the_agreement_is_busy_and_never_logged_as_no_agreement, an_erasure_the_store_refused_is_an_error_and_a_repeat_finishes_it}`, `net::tests::an_operator_list_that_cannot_be_read_lifts_nothing_and_is_asked_again`, `connectors` `tests::{a_host_list_the_store_could_not_give_at_start_lifts_nothing, a_revocation_the_store_refused_is_a_partial_request_and_a_repeat_finishes_it, an_erasure_the_worker_could_not_finish_is_noted_and_done_again_at_the_next_tick, a_retry_the_store_would_not_queue_is_a_failed_run_and_an_error_never_retrying, a_delivery_whose_run_cannot_be_closed_is_a_partial_request_and_never_nothing_applied}`), payments (`tests::{payments_that_can_never_be_refunded_do_not_starve_the_ones_that_can, a_stranded_payment_that_fails_before_the_provider_costs_nothing_against_the_few_per_sweep}`), and the class sweep (`secretbox::tests::a_pseudonym_key_the_store_would_not_give_is_an_error_and_never_a_different_key`, `queue::tests::a_job_leaves_the_queue_only_once_it_is_in_the_dead_letters`, `oauth::tests::a_state_that_could_not_be_consumed_is_not_used_and_one_the_store_would_not_give_is_not_invalid`, `connectors` `tests::{a_pull_never_adds_a_record_again_because_the_store_would_not_say_it_was_imported, a_pull_keeps_the_link_of_every_record_it_made_whichever_call_the_store_refuses}`, `gates::tests::a_rollback_does_not_skip_the_identity_check_when_the_id_of_this_database_cannot_be_read`, `backup::review_tests::a_backup_is_not_made_without_the_id_of_the_database_because_the_store_would_not_say`, `update_tests::{a_count_the_store_would_not_give_is_never_read_as_no_records, the_settings_a_change_is_compared_with_are_never_nothing_because_the_store_would_not_give_them}`); OAuth (`obop-oauth` `tests::{a_code_exchange_refused_at_any_call_is_never_a_final_invalid_grant_and_its_retry_works, a_grant_ended_while_its_access_token_could_not_be_revoked_finishes_when_the_client_comes_back}`); the code word only at the start (`fault::tests::messages_start_with_the_code_word_and_are_recognised_again`); the answer after a commit (`obop-http` `ops::tests::a_redirect_after_a_commit_is_an_answer_that_worked_and_a_partial_503_is_counted_apart`); the blob store (`pg_pool_tests::{processes_that_open_the_blob_store_together_on_a_fresh_database_all_start, a_bulk_write_makes_the_small_writes_and_reads_of_its_process_wait_and_never_busy}`, `blob::tests::the_same_bytes_uploaded_twice_at_once_are_stored_once_and_both_uploads_succeed`, now with a pool of two and 300 ms to acquire). Settings (`crates/kernel/obop-storage/src/pg_pool_tests.rs`, no server needed): `the_defaults_are_the_documented_ones` (every default as a literal); `every_setting_is_read_from_its_variable`; `a_bad_value_stops_start_up_and_names_the_variable_without_echoing_a_url` (also: a pooler in transaction mode without a lock address, or with the pooler's own address, is refused); `a_database_opened_for_a_moment_does_not_take_the_process_settings`. Against PostgreSQL (the same file, `OBOP_TEST_POSTGRES_URL`; each in a database of its own): `a_five_second_read_does_not_block_a_concurrent_get` (a `pg_sleep(5)` read on a pooled connection beside `get`: the median get is under 50 ms or five times the median of an idle get, whichever is larger; a machine busy with other work makes every get slow, with or without the read); `a_streaming_scan_holds_one_connection_and_nothing_else` (pool `in_use` is 1 and the lock pool's 0 during the scan); `a_scan_sees_one_snapshot_from_its_first_row_to_its_last` (an insert, a delete and an update from other connections between two fetches of 1,300 rows are not seen); `two_hundred_requests_on_a_pool_of_eight_refuse_the_surplus_within_the_acquire_timeout`; `a_full_queue_refuses_without_waiting_and_parks_no_more_than_its_bound` (the memory figures are in the HTTP test below); `the_lock_pool_does_not_take_interactive_capacity_and_the_other_way_round`; `nested_locks_of_one_thread_share_a_connection_so_a_small_lock_pool_cannot_deadlock`; `time_limits_follow_the_command_class_and_never_leak_between_callers` (single statements, transactions and snapshots, and the class without a limit); `a_cancelled_statement_is_recognised_by_its_code_not_by_the_words_of_the_server`; `a_statement_over_its_limit_is_cancelled_and_the_connection_stays_in_the_pool`; `a_slow_consumer_is_not_cut_off_by_the_statement_limit_but_a_stalled_one_is_ended`; `a_connection_killed_by_the_server_is_noticed_and_replaced` (health check; a read repeated once; a write fails once and the pool has healed); `batches_that_name_the_same_records_in_opposite_order_do_not_deadlock` and `a_transaction_the_server_rolled_back_for_a_deadlock_is_run_again` (with `shared_tests::concurrent_batches`, which also runs on memory and SQLite in `shared_contract`: opposite-order batches, a counter created by whoever comes first, six callers inserting one new record); `a_lock_address_that_reaches_another_database_is_refused_at_start_up`. With Docker (containers of the test's own, `obop-pool-pg-<pid>-<n>`, removed afterwards; skipped with a message when Docker or an image is missing): `after_a_server_restart_the_pool_serves_again_without_restarting_the_process` (`docker restart` of a throwaway `postgres:17` under a running pool: calls during the outage answer within 5 s with `storage_unavailable`, the pool serves again within 30 s of the database accepting connections, then writes, counters, a batch, a lock and a scan work); `behind_pgbouncer_in_transaction_mode_everything_works_with_locks_on_a_direct_address` and `pgbouncer_without_protocol_level_prepared_statements_works_too` (PgBouncer, pinned by image digest, which the test asks `SHOW VERSION` (1.26.1); `pool_mode = transaction`, two server connections, `tests::contract` plus the counter, value, lock, batch, import and export checks across two handles; the full `shared_contract` is not run through the pooler because it lets values live 50 ms, which three round trips through a container network cannot honour). A real server (`crates/obop/tests/pool.rs`): `an_exhausted_pool_answers_503_with_retry_after_never_a_401_and_the_server_recovers` (a pool of two, a table lock held by another session, 200 signed-in requests, then 800 more: all 503, `storage_busy` with `Retry-After: 1` within the timeout, `storage_timeout` with `Retry-After: 5` for the ones that held the connections, never 401 or 400; the server's memory read throughout; `/healthz` up; `/readyz` ready while both connections are held; the series on `/metrics`; served again after the lock is released, without a restart); `a_create_is_never_answered_503_after_it_committed_and_never_stored_after_a_503` (a pool of one and no queue, 360 creates beside a background reader: every 200 is stored, every 503 `none` is not; that a refusal after a commit keeps its answer is not forced over HTTP, where it depends on who gets the one connection, but in `ops::tests::a_request_that_committed_a_change_keeps_its_answer` and `a_redirect_after_a_commit_is_an_answer_that_worked_and_a_partial_503_is_counted_apart`); and `a_bad_pool_setting_stops_the_server_at_start_up_and_names_the_variable`. Backoffice: `serverMessage.test.ts` (the three code words as sentences in every language and in English, and `storageFaultMessage` for the sign-in form), `net.test.ts` (`Retry-After` is waited for; a timed-out statement is not read again at once). **Unchanged and green:** `tests::postgres_meets_contract_when_available`, `shared_tests::postgres_shared_contract_between_two_connections` (apart from the timing note below), `shared_tests::a_panicking_holder_releases_its_lock`, `blob::tests::postgres_bytes_meet_the_contract_when_available`, the TLS tests (one opens its backend with a longer acquire timeout, because containers started in parallel on a busy machine need more than two seconds for a first TLS handshake) and `crates/obop/tests/multi_process.rs`. The visibility of `tests::contract` and `shared_tests::shared_contract` changed (`pub(crate)`), so the pooler tests can call them.

**Consequences.** *Measured on one Windows 11 machine with Docker Desktop, PostgreSQL 17, a debug build of obop with optimised dependencies, other work running on the machine (numbers vary by a factor of ten with that load):* a `pg_sleep(5)` read beside `get`: 49 to 58 gets in 3 s, median 2 to 6 ms (idle median 1 to 12 ms), worst 3 to 160 ms. Pool 8, 200 concurrent 3 s requests: 8 served, 192 `storage_busy` after 2.01 to 2.19 s. Queue bound 5, 300 callers on 2 busy connections: peak waiting 5, 295 refused at once. Over HTTP (pool 2, acquire timeout 0.5 s, every statement blocked by a table lock): 193 to 198 `storage_busy` (the slowest after 0.5 to 0.9 s), 2 to 7 `storage_timeout`. The server process (`obop serve`) was 21 MiB resident idle, 25 MiB at the peak of a 200-request stampede on a pool of two and 27 to 29 MiB at the peak of one with 800 requests. A restarted PostgreSQL container: the first refused call 0.9 to 14 s after the restart began, refused calls answered within 2.0 s (the acquire timeout) at most, and the pool served within 0.2 s of the database accepting connections again (the container itself needed 6 to 44 s). Sixteen threads taking three nested locks five times on a lock pool of two: 0.6 to 1.0 s. Behind PgBouncer eight client connections shared two server connections. *Timing in the existing tests:* `shared_tests::shared_contract` lets counters and values live 50 to 120 ms and expects two consecutive calls to fall inside that; each call is a round trip to the container, so on a machine where a write takes 100 ms or more (measured here during heavy builds: a `counter_add` took 0.2 to 1.0 s on the shared test database while a `SELECT` took 1 ms, with or without the pool) it fails; the pooled backend adds no round trip to a call. *Costs.* One more `SET` when the class changes between single statements; two more round trips per call in transaction-pooler mode; up to 20 connections per process against `max_connections`; the idle-connection ping adds one round trip to a call that follows a pause of a second or more (`OBOP_DB_HEALTH_CHECK_IDLE_MS`); an atomic batch takes one advisory-lock statement before its steps (at most 128 locks, and a bulk write makes the other atomic batches of the database wait while it runs); the scan limit is per fetch, so a scan over a very large model is bounded by the idle-in-transaction limit and not by the 15 s class (an aggregate that needs longer than 15 s per 500 rows is cancelled). *Behaviour that differs from before, deliberately:* a storage fault (full pool, unreachable database, statement over its limit) answers 503 with `Retry-After` instead of a 400 with the driver's text or a 401 from a swallowed error, **saying whether anything had been changed (`applied`); a request that committed and lost only something optional keeps its answer, and one that lost a step it needed is a 503 `partial`** (see the rule above; a request can still succeed while its audit entry is lost after four tries, which is counted and logged); after a refused call a request makes no further change; a call that used to wait behind a long read now runs beside it; writes of one process run at the same moment instead of one after the other (see "Batches"); a scan or an export is one repeatable-read transaction (before: one statement, which also saw one snapshot, but on the connection everyone else waited for); a ninth simultaneous lock-holding thread is refused after two seconds (before: a new connection); error texts of the database carry SQLSTATE and message (which also makes `explain_db_error`, which matches `obop_uq_` in the text, work against PostgreSQL). Command semantics are otherwise those of the contract tests, which pass unchanged on memory, SQLite and PostgreSQL.

*Not delivered.* **Left open after the third review, with reasons:** a person's own password change cannot be finished by a repeat (the repeat needs the old password) and keeps no note, so the log says to sign out everywhere; the refusal of a plain claim of the shop's key is answered at once and the buyer submits again, there is no queue for it; a plan that cannot be put back within two seconds is lost (logged, and the answer says so; the plan id goes to stderr when the log is refused too); the cross-process queue behind a bulk write (above). **Left open after the fifth round (the fourth re-check), each with its sequence and why it is rare:** (1) a plan put back after a refused run whose own put-back write failed with `storage_unavailable` may in fact have been stored, so the log says "lost" while a live copy waits for its time limit (the approver is told to ask again, and the old copy stays approvable until its time limit, so approving both the old and the new plan would run the action twice; it needs the put-back to fail with an unknown outcome right after a refused run, and both approvals by people who were told the first is lost); (2) a flow row for a flow that never left (the row is written before the run starts, so a run that cannot be recorded stops the flow but leaves a `released` row that counts toward the rate and volume caps: conservative); (3) a stale `user_disable` note after a disable whose outcome was unknown and which did not commit (enabling an account that was never disabled then signs out its sessions older than the note, once); (4) a stranded payment whose refund record stays refused for more than a day (the provider's idempotency key lives one day, so the sweep's repeat may be refused as "already refunded" and the payment stays `succeeded`, and no action records a refund that was made in the provider's dashboard, so the record is corrected in the database; a payment that failed is left alone for an hour and five provider calls at most are made per sweep, inside the checkout that started the sweep); (5) the recovery codes of a first passkey enrolment whose session limit could not be lifted are in an answer that became a 503 (they exist and work; the person makes new ones under Security); (6) a `refund` or `refund_delivered` whose money went back and whose payment record was refused leaves the order paid and the payment succeeded until staff repeat it (the repeat is safe), while a cancel is order-first: the two are not the same order of steps because a paid order cannot be marked paid again, which a pending one can. **Left open after the sixth round (the second re-check and the class sweep):** (1) the sweep's refund of a stranded payment is per process (the hour of waiting is kept in memory): two processes may each try a payment that cannot be refunded once an hour, and a restart tries it again at once; (2) `installed_manifests` and the list of installed packages at start-up read a refused list as "no packages installed": a command line or a start that meets that registers nothing until the next start (fail closed for the commands, but nothing says so; a request scope answers 503); (3) `limit::hit`, the rate limiter, lets a hit through when its counter cannot be written (a limiter must not take the service down with the database): in a request scope the layer then answers 503 anyway, so this matters only to code that calls it off a request, and none does; (4) the in-memory `Auth` reads that answer a plain value (`is_disabled`, `must_change_password`, `mfa_enabled`, `security_setting`, `passkeys_of`) read a refusal as "no" and are protected by the guard in a request scope and by `settled` where they feed a write; a command that called them off a request would not be, and none does; (5) the community's forum and status caches keep their old data when a refresh is refused (a page can be stale for the length of the refusal); (6) `external_change` after a delivery cannot be isolated by a test, because the flow-log row is already a change of the request: it is defence in depth, and the test asserts the `partial` that the end-of-run step gives. Per-person fairness or admission control above the pool; a FIFO queue (waiters wake in the operating system's order); a SQLite reader connection; an asynchronous `Backend`; pools for the control plane's single-shot connections (the cell admin connection and the status probe still open one connection each through `PgConnector`); a lease limit on a held connection (a consumer that never returns holds its connection until its process ends, visible as `obop_db_pool_in_use`; a scan's snapshot is ended by the server after two minutes of idleness). **Deferred after the review, with reasons:** (a) a lock pool larger than 8 (the requirement says at most 8; an MCP request holds one lock connection for its whole duration, so a ninth concurrent MCP session request in one process gets `storage_busy`; raising the cap or not pinning a session for the whole request is the owner's call); (b) a 503 for the operations of the OAuth endpoints and the public pages in OpenAPI (they are not in the documents; the OAuth endpoints answer an OAuth error with `Retry-After`, the pages a small page); every operation that the two documents (control plane and instance) list declares it, with `Retry-After`, `X-Obop-Applied` and the `applied` field; (c) a read-through path for post-commit work other than the create and update read-back (observers of the dispatcher, workflow actions' follow-ups) beyond the guard's rule that a committed request keeps its answer; (d) the `bulk_create`-style idempotency key for single creates and updates, which would make the one remaining case (`storage_unavailable` after a change in flight) safe to repeat. **Not tried** against a managed PostgreSQL service or pooler, PgBouncer in session mode, any PgBouncer other than 1.26.1, PostgreSQL 18, Linux (the tests ran on Windows with Docker Desktop, where `TCP_USER_TIMEOUT` has no effect), a server with a non-English `lc_messages` (the classification by SQLSTATE is tested with a raised error that carries a German message), a network partition that drops packets silently, a failover to a replica, a sustained load of minutes or a pool of 64, or pool and TLS together beyond the one D66 test; and the memory figure is one process observed on one machine, not a proof of absence of growth.

**Review.** The branch went through an adversarial review (43 findings; 26 confirmed by two verifiers, 11 disputed, the rest refuted). What changed: the 503 rule and the guard (hygiene-1, faults-1, poolcore-7, pgbackend-4); the vault read (faults-3, pgbackend-3); transaction mode needs a lock address (poolcore-1, pgbackend-6, hygiene-3); batches order their locks and deadlocks are rerun (pgbackend-1, pgbackend-2); OAuth refresh (faults-2); control plane and community routes (faults-4, hygiene-2); background threads and egress (faults-5); the 503's shape and headers (faults-7); a limit of 0 in transactions and snapshots (poolcore-2, pgbackend-5); SQLSTATE for cancelled statements (poolcore-6); a panic while connecting (poolcore-4); retry after the backoff (poolcore-5); the seeded connection (poolcore-8); the bounded ping (poolcore-9); the identity check by simple query (poolcore-3); `/readyz` when the pool is full (regression-2); the sign-in form and the retry timing (faults-6, faults-8); `OBOP_DB_*` and the lock address no longer reach other databases opened by the control plane (regression-4, hygiene-7); URL options win over the pool's defaults (pgbackend-8); the PgBouncer image is pinned and asked for its version, the site wording says what was tried (hygiene-6); the test hang (regression-5) and the tests that claimed more than they asserted (poolcore-10, pgbackend-9, hygiene-5). **Second round.** Three reviewers found that the 503 rule of the first revision still let a request finish half done without saying so: a webhook answered 200 after its event was not applied, blob holds lost after a create, the revoke of a token the request had issued refused by the guard that was meant to protect it, offboarding that reported "0 sessions ended" when the token list was refused, a refused account lookup answered "no such user", a retention run that deleted by an empty list of connections, OAuth 503s without `Retry-After`, a thousand-row batch that took a thousand locks, two uploads of one file that failed on the primary key, and a maintenance command refused in a transaction-pooler layout. The rule is now the three cases above (none or unknown; partial; kept only for what nobody needs), each of the fixes has a test that fails without it (listed under the acceptance tests above), the testing backend (`Flaky`) is behind the `test-support` feature of `obop-storage` and not in the production crate, and the readiness test waits for the pool to be full instead of sleeping a guessed 400 ms. **Fifth round.** A fourth re-check of the money, revocation and approval fixes confirmed twenty findings, all but the ones above fixed with a test that fails without the fix: an `update` of a governed state ran the bare action and skipped the refund, close-payment and stock wrappers (money-1); the sweep never looked at a cancelled order whose payment succeeded (money-2); a refund of delivered goods left a window in which the sweep would shelve the goods (money-3); a refused refund record left a pending order that could be marked paid (money-4); a release that guessed after a refused read, and a claim that took a vanished key for its own (money-5, money-6, fault-6); the sweep's refusals in the buyer's request (money-7); a passkey-only person's repeated reset left the recovery codes, one note slot overwritten by a later change, and a note taken back after an unknown outcome (revocation-1, -2, -3); a second step of a sign-in for an account disabled in between (revocation-4); a log line that named the wrong remedy and a swallowed enrolment-limit refusal (revocation-5, -7); a plan lost where the layer says "none", and put back where the outcome is unknown (fault-2, -3, revocation-6); a reset's note left after a refusal that changed nothing (fault-7); a connection whose rollback failed handed to the rerun after a deadlock (fault-5); and connector records that left with no trace and an answer of "none" after a delivery (fault-1). **Sixth round.** A second re-check of the fifth round confirmed fifteen findings, fixed with a test that fails without the fix: a repeated offboarding refused at its own write erased the note of the first (the earlier note is put back); a half-written mask vault entry was reused on the retry and its token sent with nothing to restore it (the entry restore reads is written first and mended); the provider's agreement, the operator's host allow-list, erasure and revocation read a refusal as "no agreement", "no restriction" and "done"; a retry the store would not queue left a run "retrying" for ever; two-factor enrolment threw away a refused lift of its session's limit; a password change wrote a limit computed from a policy read that had been refused; a sign-in without a second step could issue a session after a disable had run; five payments that can never be refunded starved the sixth; a plan was dropped when no connection could be opened at all; and two tests could not fail for the reason they claimed. **Class sweep.** The grep of the pattern (a refused call read as a default) over the store layer and the kernel crates, and what it found, are in the rule above. **Evidence.** D44, D59, D66, D67, docs/DEPLOY.md "Connection pool and poolers", the PostgreSQL documentation for `statement_timeout`, `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` and advisory locks, the PgBouncer documentation for transaction pooling, the tests above.

#### D79 Persons, organisation, grants and scoped row rules
**Status** PROPOSED (owner-directed in chat, 2026-10-25/26, as item 2 of the plan, "org units and scoped row rules, decision record first"; not yet ratified as binding). **Reversibility** the code is a two-way door (two new built-in modules, optional manifest keys, new commands, additive fields on existing records; a manifest, an account or a user that uses none of it behaves as before). Four things are one-way doors once an instance holds data in them: (1) a minted person id, because it is also the account key and the value that `own`, `shared_with` and grants hold; (2) the stored shapes of `access_role` and `access_grant` (options may be added, a field is never retyped or removed); (3) the manifest keys a package starts to use (a process older than this record cannot load such a package and treats the module as absent, which fails closed); (4) the three security fixes that change behaviour by default (blob reads, computed totals and effect children follow row visibility). **Owner action** yes, four small items, none of which blocks starting work: (a) name the reviewer for the security paths (`obop-auth`, `obop-oauth`, the authority and gate code), so `.github/CODEOWNERS` can be written; (b) confirm the sign-in alias design, which touches the sign-in path; (c) accept before the gate and channel work packages merge that a readable-but-not-allowed refusal becomes HTTP 403, that effects, blobs and computed totals follow visibility, and that an approval re-resolves its owner live; (d) read the 25 waiver texts for bundled models in the work package that adds them. At deployment time (no deploy is approved by this record) the operator sets `OBOP_ADMIN_BYPASS=0` and `OBOP_DISCLOSE_WITHHELD=0` on new hosted instances; both default to 1, today's behaviour.

**Source.** Derived from the requirement set of the first downstream project, kept outside this repository (identity, tenancy and authorisation: persons and organisation, scoped grants and their administration, row rules and their side channels, the install-time proof), and from the defect register and two architecture records of that project's first module, which show what obop could not yet express. Where the requirement text contradicts the repository, the repository wins and Consequences lists the deviations. Nothing here names that project, a customer or an industry; examples use generic units and sites. Read with D10 (the algebra this record implements: grants OR, restrictions AND, default-deny), D18, D40, D47, D48, D53, D56 to D58, D64, D65, D69, D70 and D72, and with the step 1 records D73 to D78 (see Consequences for the order of merging).

**Decision.**

*1. Shape.* One decision, three topics, one set of shared contracts. Two new built-in modules: `org` (persons and organisation) and `access` (roles, grants and the explorer, in the new kernel crate `obop-access`). An extension of the existing `[model.access]` table. A person-aware account path in `obop-auth`. One test kit crate. Every command is generated or declared in the registry and therefore on REST, MCP, the CLI, OpenAPI and `/api/meta` with a screen in the backoffice in the same change (UI parity is literal); the only hand-written routes are the D69 account routes, which gain fields and two siblings.

*2. Who is a person.* A person is a record of `org_person`. Its id is the principal id everywhere: the `auth_user` key, `Principal.id`, `Actor.id`, the value in `own` and `shared_with`, and `access_grant.person`. An id is minted server side (`PersonId::mint()`, a lower case UUIDv7; an `id` sent by a client is ignored) or adopted once from an existing account key (frozen from then on; one source file may call `PersonId::adopted`, which refuses a `bg-`, `svc-` or `connector:` prefix and any id with a `/`). Nothing derives an id from an e-mail address, a principal name, a name or an IdP subject. Credentials stay in `auth_user`; the link is key equality; a person with no account (a contractor, a mailbox, a historical person) and an account with no person (a legacy account until it is adopted) are both legal. People type a sign-in alias, not the id: `auth_login/<alias>` maps a lower case alias (`[a-z0-9._@-]{1,64}`, never UUID-shaped, never equal to any account key or alias) to the account key, is resolved in `login_step_impl`, the one door that takes typed text, and the lockout counter is keyed by the resolved key. Renaming an alias changes no token, grant, passkey or `own` value.

*3. Creating, adopting, leaving.* D69's path stays the only way to make an account. `create_user_as`, `set_groups_as`, `offboard_as` and the other creation paths first become one `apply_checked` batch of the change and its security event (today the change and the event are separate writes); person-aware creation then composes, in that one batch, the ops of the person row (built by the `PersonRegistry` port that `org` implements), the account insert, the alias insert and the event. There is no `org_person.invite` command, because a generated temporary password cannot leave the Dispatcher. `adopt` turns an existing account into a person with id = its key (`obop admin adopt NAME | --all [--dry-run]`, `user_account.adopt`, REST `POST /api/auth/users/{id}/adopt`). Account creation, adoption and alias changes need `platform.admin`; `org.person_admin` manages profile, lifecycle, memberships and identifiers, not credentials. Person status is governed (D48): `invited`, `active`, `suspended`, `left`, moved by `activate`, `suspend`, `reinstate`, `leave` and `rehire`, each needs approval from an agent. `leave` is a resumable, fail-closed sequence: mark the person `suspended` with `leave_pending`, run D69's offboarding (which also ends the person's grants in its own atomic batch), then in one batch end every membership and identifier at `left_on`, write a leaver run and one counted ownership task per (model, `own` field) that still holds the id, and bump the epoch; running it again after any crash completes it and equals an uninterrupted run. An account bound to a person refuses the generic `disable`, `enable` and `offboard` on every surface (the operator CLI keeps `--force`, which writes a security event). `org_ownership_task.reassign` rewrites an owner field, which is a read escalation (`own` grants read and write), so it needs `platform.admin`.

*4. The organisation is data.* `org_level` (the vocabulary: id, rank, allowed parent levels, at most one `stamp` level; `seed_defaults` offers country, region, office, unit, site), `org_node` (a node is closed, never deleted), `org_node_edge` (the effective-dated truth: half-open `[valid_from, valid_to)` in `date` kind, one parent per child on every date, no cycle on any date, checked at every later edge start so a future-dated move cannot close one), `org_node_alias`, `org_company`, `org_company_relation`, `org_node_operator`. `org_membership`: many per person, one `main` on any date, effective-dated, and a membership grants nothing. There is no stored mirror of the current parent or main node (it would go stale at a date boundary with no scheduler to refresh it); as-of reads return them. Invariants are refused with a message that names them. Structure changes only through module commands: generic create, update, delete and `bulk_create` are refused for everyone, `platform.admin` included, on every model the index holds, and every batch that changes levels, nodes, edges, memberships or operators carries `authority::bump_op()` (decision 12). The engine sees the tree through one port, `OrgDirectory` (as-of ancestors, descendants, stamp node, operator, resolve, existence, the next date boundary, display names), implemented by an in-memory `OrgIndex` rebuilt when the permission epoch changes. A storage error, an unreadable index or a corrupt tree (a cycle, two parents) never widens anything: the call returns an error, or the nodes involved are detached from every set and listed by `org_problem`. A node set is the descendants as of today; a closed node drops out of every set, so its records are visible to unscoped holders only until it is reopened or its records are moved, and `org_node.close` reports how many records are stamped with it.

*5. Identifiers, matching, feed v0.* `org_external_identifier` keeps (kind, namespace, folded value) with a unique `active_key` for active rows; a pure matcher tries principal name, mail, mail alias and employee number in that order and never a name; a conflict opens an `org_match_case`, a `locked` identifier is never changed automatically, every decision writes an audit row that holds ids and not values. `org_field_source.apply_feed` is one idempotent command with per-field provenance (a field a human typed is never overwritten), at most 1,000 facts, `dry_run`. Connectors and a scheduler are not part of this record.

*6. One rule language.* Row rules stay data on `[model.access]` and stay closed (`deny_unknown_fields`). The five D58 keys keep their meaning exactly. There are four positions: READ, WRITE, DELETE and one per action. A position is a list of grants that OR together and a list of restrictions that AND after them; `WRITE = READ and w`, `DELETE = READ and d` (else WRITE), `ACTION(a) = READ and row_access(a)` (else WRITE), so a write rule is never wider than read and there is no blind write. New grants: `scope` (`record[field]` in the caller's node set for a group, or equal to the caller's stamp node), `related` (a member row of another model links the caller to the record by user, node or group, with optional roles; capped at 500 user and 200 node or group member rows per record, enforced by generated guards). New restriction: `restrict = [{ expr, unless = ["own" | group] }]`, an expression limited to the closed subset `true false ! && || is_set(F)` and `==`/`!=` of a stored text, select, ref or date field against a literal. Other keys: `[model.access.write]`, `[model.access.delete]`, per-action `row_access`, `[model.aggregate]`, `agent_writes = "propose"`, `create = "effects"` (no generated `create` or `bulk_create`), `access_waiver`. Field control: `write_group` (who may change the field), `read_when` and `write_when` (relations `own`, `scope`, `any`; a field with a non-`any` condition is not filterable, sortable or aggregable), `action_only = true` (written only by effects of declared actions; named so it cannot be confused with D75's reserved system fields), `computed_visibility = "all"` (keeps the old sum over every child). Any missing, null, empty or mistyped value is false, never an error. The `shared_with` list uses the member tokenizer of D78 (`keyexpr::member_tokens`, one constant for Rust and SQL), not the four separators of today's text.

*7. Caller facts.* One type carries them: `Reach { stamp, sets, epoch, resolved }` on `Principal` (an `Arc`) and on `Actor`. `sets` maps a group to a node set and holds only groups the caller has through scoped grants alone; a held group absent from `sets` is held everywhere (direct groups and unscoped grants), so `Principal.groups` keeps meaning "held in any scope" and `allows` is unchanged. `stamp` is the person's main-membership node lifted to the stamp level, as of today (None when there is none). A principal built without resolving a reach (a connector, an observer, a test) is `resolved = false`, and every `scope` grant is false for it: a scoped group can never turn into an unscoped one by being copied. For a `scope` rule the evaluator reads the caller as follows: group not held, false; held in `sets`, the record's field must be in the set; held and not in `sets`, true for every record, stamped or not. A holder of a scoped root grant sees the records stamped with an existing node and no record with an empty node (pinned by a test). `see_all_group`, `all_group` and every bypass-like rule use `holds_everywhere` (an unscoped hold or the superuser), never the any-scope `holds`. A holder of a model's read group through scoped grants alone, on a model with no `scope` entry for that group, sees no row by the group route (`own`, `shared_with`, `see_all` still apply): a scoped grant can never become an unscoped one by landing on a model that has no scope rule. Reach is stamped into a call as `_actor_reach` and `_actor_approver` (the `_actor` prefix is already stripped from client input), only for the groups some installed `scope` or `related` rule names. Caps, each fail-closed: an expansion of one grant target is refused above 5,000 nodes, a set holds at most 10,000 nodes and a caller at most 50 groups. An agent's reach is its owner's, narrowed to the groups its token scopes allow, so what an agent can read is a subset of what its owner can read.

*8. Order and wording of the checks.* (1) the command group (`p.allows`, any-scope hold; none: 404 as today); (2) guards before the handler: a filter or order on a field the caller cannot read, then the governed-field, action and delete guards and a new `row_guard` that loads the record with READ and then the position (so an agent's proposal fails at proposal time); (3) the handler: READ on the record (hidden: `no <model> <id>`, status 400, unchanged and byte-identical to a missing record), then the position (readable but not allowed: `CallError::Denied`, REST 403, MCP `isError` with `denied`, CLI exit 3, text `<model> <id> cannot be changed by you`, the same for update, delete and action), then field rules, then kinds and refs (a ref needs READ of its target), assertions, unique values, sequences; (4) `create`: stamp every empty `scope` field with the caller's stamp, check the node (a non-empty scope value that is supplied on create or changed on update must equal the stamp or lie in a set for a scope rule on that field, so an owner cannot stamp a foreign unit; the superuser passes), then WRITE and READ on the candidate; (5) `update`: WRITE before and WRITE after (a move needs the group at both nodes; the caller cannot lose write on the result). Create and update preparation moves into one function (`engine/write.rs`, used by `create`, `bulk_create` and effects), so one door applies stamping, field rules and positions. `list` and `search` use READ only: results, totals and pages are identical with and without a write block. `Gate` stays the one predicate holder: `Gate::decide(def, pos, id, rec, trace) -> Decision` returns the verdict and the reasons, `allows` is `decide` without a trace, `visible` is `allows(Read)`, and the permission explorer calls the same function. Raw store reads are renamed `raw_get`, `raw_find`, `raw_all`, `raw_find_in`; a register file lists every source file that calls one outside tests with its purpose and count, and a test fails on a new call site.

*9. Side channels.* Each has a decision and a named test. S1 a blob is readable when some record that holds it passes READ and the field's group and `read_when`; none: the same not-found as a missing blob. S2 computed totals count the children the caller can see on every read path (`computed_visibility = "all"` restores the old sum for a field), while assertions and rule evaluation read raw. S3 a unique value stays global unless `unique_with` names the scope field, and its message never names a record. S4 `Response.denied` is emptied unless `OBOP_DISCLOSE_WITHHELD` is on, withheld fields are omitted silently, `/api/meta` gets `fields_withheld`. S5 counts, totals and pages are READ-filtered. S6 hidden and missing are one message builder, byte-equal on every surface (timing is measured, not claimed). S7 effects (`for_each`, children targets, `match` locators) read visible rows only, hidden ones are skipped silently and do not count toward the 500 cap; `for_each ... where` and `match ... require = "one"` exist; there is no escape until service principals exist. S8 connector principals are rebuilt live from the owner (decision 11). S9 discovery (meta, commands, OpenAPI, MCP tool list) names no withheld field and never exposes a restrict expression. S10 `bulk_create` runs the checks of `create` per row; seeds, restore and imports by the store are unchecked and the spec says so. S11 no audit event carries row data. S12 the reads register. Accepted residuals, each pinned by a test: a refused duplicate says that a (possibly hidden) record holds the value, a refused delete says that records the caller cannot see refer to it, assertions read all children, sequences have gaps.

*10. Aggregate-only access.* `[model.aggregate]` generates `<model>.aggregate` with its own permission group (it is not an overload of `search`, which has one group per command). Input is limited to declared dimensions and measures (`count`, `sum`, `avg`; no `min` or `max`, which show single values); a date dimension needs a bucket at or above `min_bucket`; the rows are those passing READ restricts and `aggregate.scope`, whatever the caller's READ. `engine::aggregate_only::suppress` is the only suppression code, used by every surface: groups with fewer distinct `cohort_field` values than `min_cohort` (default 5, never below 2) merge into one `suppressed` bucket, a single fallen group takes the smallest remaining one with it (`secondary: true`), no total is returned, calls are rate limited per principal and model and logged with a fingerprint and no data. Dimensions may not be personal or sensitive fields. Differencing across overlapping queries is not prevented and product text must not claim it is.

*11. Grants and roles (module `access`).* Authority is data. `access_role` (id `module.role`; groups split into unscoped and scoped lists; `scope` none, node or company; `approvals` 1 to 3 frozen into each grant and at least 2 when the bundle holds a reserved group; `may_grant`; status) and `access_grant` (person, role, target node or company id, `descendants`, `valid_from` and `valid_to` as `datetime`, state `proposed`, `active`, `ended` or `rejected`, basis, approvers, `open_key` made unique with `ensure_unique` so one open grant per person, role and target). Both are read-only generated models; every change is a declared command with a bound handler. `auth_user.groups` stay as direct groups: unscoped, undated, permanent. A person's effective groups are direct groups plus the groups of live grants, and an unscoped hold of a group removes it from the scoped map. A user with no grants resolves exactly as today, so there is no flag day. Instances move person by person, or all at once with `access_batch.adopt_groups` (a digest-checked dry run, then one grant per account and non-reserved direct group under role `legacy.<group>`), and may then switch `security.direct_groups` to `reserved_only` (opt-in per instance; refused while an enabled account would lose a group it holds only directly). Reserved: the prefixes `platform.`, `access.`, `security.`, `audit.`, `privacy.` and the three exact names `org.person_admin`, `org.node_admin`, `org.feed_operator`; a reserved group in a bundle forces `approvals >= 2` and removes the role from every `may_grant`; `platform.admin` is never in a bundle. The `org` module ships the role `org.member` (bundle `org.staff`) so a person always has a role to be granted once direct groups are retired. Lifecycle: `propose` (agents may propose), `confirm` (counted approvals; humans only; a fresh session; not the grantee or the proposer when two approvals are needed; separation of duties over the resulting explicit groups; delegation only through `may_grant` and only inside the delegate's own node set; a grant that cannot be covered answers `no access_grant <id>` as for a missing record), `reject`, `end`, `shorten`, `sweep`, `import` (CSV, dry run first, at most 1,000 rows), a cover grant (`basis = cover`) of at most 90 days; nobody can propose a grant for themselves or for their own agent; the last unscoped holder of `access.role_admin` or `access.grant_admin` cannot be ended. Freshness is session age (`security.step_up_secs`, 0 to 3,600, 900 on new instances, the error `reauth_required`) until a real step-up exists. Connector principals are rebuilt at each run from the owner's live principal, intersected with the model's read group; a disabled or offboarded owner makes the flow refuse.

*12. One epoch, one resolver, one cache.* The permission epoch is the record `obop_meta/permission_epoch`, bumped with `Op::add(...).or_create(...)` in the same `apply_checked` batch as every change that can alter who holds what: grant confirm, end, shorten, sweep, adopt; role groups, authority or retirement; package role sync; `end_grants_of`; a change of the `security` settings; and every write that changes the organisation (levels, nodes, edges, memberships, operators). The tree and the grants share the one counter because a grant's node set is computed from the tree: two counters would let one go stale. There is no separate organisation epoch. `Authority::effective(person, now)` is the only resolver: it reads the epoch once per request, uses one generation per epoch per process (all active grants indexed by person, the role catalogue, memoised node sets, per-person results valid until the next grant, edge or membership boundary, the boundary being midnight in the platform zone), rebuilds under a single-flight lock, and fails closed: an epoch read or generation build error means no principal (401), a missing node gives a held group with an empty set. `OBOP_ACCESS_EPOCH_CACHE_MS` (0 to 5,000, default 0, start-up refuses more) trades epoch reads for bounded staleness. After a restore the epoch may move backwards; caches compare with `!=`. With the default setting a request that comes after a commit in any process sees it: a revoke is effective on the next request.

*13. The log and the explorer.* `security_event` is the change log. It gains optional `subject`, `ref`, `before`, `after`, `retention`, its read group becomes `audit.reader` (`platform.admin` still satisfies it), and authority commands write change, bump and event in one batch (`audit::event_op`). `access_decision.explain` evaluates one person's current principal on a model, an operation and **supplied candidate fields** (it reads no stored record, so it is no existence oracle) and returns groups with provenance, the scope sets, the matching reasons, what would have granted it, and field outcomes; naming a stored record id instead needs `platform.admin` and a fresh session and is logged. `who_can` (audit.reader, fresh, at most 500 ids), `mine`, `can` (which of up to 200 readable ids the caller may update, delete or act on) and the virtual model `access_rule` (the rules per model as data and sentences, lints, waivers) complete the surface; each use of the first two is an event.

*14. The install proof.* One module, `obop_registry::proof`, runs the lints L1 to L19 (rules, waivers, roles, scoped bundles, groups, expressions, effects, aggregates, e-mail-shaped literals) from the installer, `obop modules check` and a test over the built-in modules, never from `check_module` or `to_module`, so a module already installed can never vanish at the next start. The instance setting `security.access_gate` selects `enforce` (new instances) or `warn` (migrated instances) for the lints that may be downgraded; the structural ones (a waiver on a model that has rules, `public_field` or `shared_with` with sensitive fields, e-mail literals, reserved or foreign groups in a role) block in both modes, and `modules check --strict` raises all. A model with personal or sensitive fields and neither rules nor a waiver (40 to 400 characters) fails L1; a waiver is shown in `/api/meta`, the install plan and the install event; a waiver does not excuse a sensitive field without its own group, so a model with a special-category field is accepted with a waiver only when each such field has an explicit field group. The 25 bundled models that carry such fields and have no rules (in 16 of the 22 packages) get waivers with honest texts in this change and change no behaviour.

*15. Operator, agents, approvals.* One flag, `OBOP_ADMIN_BYPASS` (default 1), read once into `Principal.admin_bypass`. `Principal::superuser()` (bypass on and `platform.admin` held) is the single bypass point for group checks and row rules; `Principal::is_admin_account()` is the group test for administrative gates (account, people, MFA, passkeys, grants). With the flag off, `platform.admin` passes row rules like anyone and holds only groups it is given plus the commands registered to it. A guard test fails when a non-test use of `ADMIN_GROUP` appears outside an allow-list. Agents: the owner's id, groups and reach narrowed by token scopes; `agent_writes = "propose"` makes `create`, `bulk_create` and `update` need approval and an action that writes such a model too. `Dispatcher::approve` rebuilds the run principal from the owner's current principal intersected with the stored plan (groups and node sets, token scopes kept); a disabled, offboarded or missing owner voids the plan; the approver must hold the command group and pass a row check on the record the plan names; `$approver` is available in effect mappings; every approval writes `plan_approve`.

*16. Storage and compilation.* The same code runs on memory, SQLite and PostgreSQL. A `Policy` is compiled lazily from `Access`; `specialise(def, pos, caller)` folds it for one caller into a `Residual` (`True`, `False`, `And`, `Or`, `Eq`, `In`, `ListHas`, `Flag`, `Cond`, `Parent`, `Member`) with every caller fact bound as a constant. The in-memory `Gate` and D76's SQL lowering both consume `Residual`; D76 adds `Pred::Member` and the SQL forms (`= ANY($n::text[])`, NULL-safe leaves, computed flags relaxed to TRUE and re-checked by the gate), and a registry test fails when a `Residual` variant has no vector in `tests/access/vectors.json` that D76's compiler must also pass. As-of reads of the organisation are plain text comparisons of canonical dates, never a recursive query over JSON: the node set arrives as one bind array from `OrgDirectory`. Unique indexes (`access_grant.open_key`, `org_external_identifier.active_key`, node `key` with level, company `registration_no`) go through `Backend::ensure_unique`; module writes bypass the engine's `check_unique`, so the module re-checks under the write lock and the index is the backstop. `Backend::find_in(model, field, values)` is owned by D76 (a default that lists and filters until then).

*17. Not a second system.* There is no second policy store (no `access_policy` records), no separate scope-source port beside `OrgDirectory`, no second epoch, no second oracle: one rule grammar, one port to the organisation, one epoch, one resolver, one decision function and one test kit.

**Threat model.** *Assets:* the person registry and its personal data; the tree; the authority to grant; the rules; the visibility of every record; the log. *A stolen or over-reaching agent:* it can propose and read within its owner's narrowed reach; `confirm`, role commands, batch apply and adopt refuse agents; its approval re-resolves the owner live and is void for a revoked owner (`agent_of_revoked_owner_gets_nothing`, `plan_is_reresolved_at_approval`). *Privilege escalation through grants:* self-grant, self-approval through an agent label, four-eyes with one person twice, delegation outside the delegate's node set, cover grants that never end, a reserved group smuggled into a bundle with holders, `platform.admin` in a bundle, a stale session confirming (`self_grant_self_approval_and_own_agent_refused`, `four_eyes_needs_two_distinct_non_proposers`, `delegated_confirm_outside_scope_answers_as_missing`, `reserved_group_cannot_join_a_role_with_holders`, `stale_session_needs_reauth_and_agents_cannot_confirm`). *Stale authority:* the epoch is in the same batch as the change, read once per request, compared with `!=`; a clock skew shifts a boundary by the skew but revocation is a write, so skew never extends it (`two_processes_honour_a_revoke_on_the_next_request_100_of_100`, `restore_to_lower_epoch_rebuilds`). *Identity confusion:* an id derived from a mail address, an alias that shadows a key, two processes claiming one alias, alternating alias and id to double the guesses, a generic disable that drifts the status (`auth_login::alias_cannot_shadow_a_key_or_look_like_an_id`, `auth_login::lockout_is_keyed_by_the_resolved_key`, `lifecycle::person_bound_account_refuses_generic_disable_enable_offboard`). *Reading across scopes by a side channel:* blobs, totals, effects, counts, pages, unique and delete messages, discovery, error text, aggregates, bulk; a world-pair test changes only what a persona cannot see and demands byte-identical responses. *Aggregate differencing and small cohorts:* suppression with a secondary bucket, no total, rate limit; differencing is documented as not prevented. *A corrupt or racing tree:* a future-dated move cannot close a cycle, two writers cannot leave two open edges (compare-and-set), a bad tree is detached and reported. *Feed poisoning and false matches:* human fields and locked identifiers survive a feed, a name is never an input to the matcher, an ambiguous match opens a case. *Mass reassignment as a read escalation:* `platform.admin` only, at most 500 rows per call. *A half-done leave:* resumable and idempotent. *A scoped group widened by a code path that copies group text* (connector principals): unresolved reach makes scope grants false until the principal is rebuilt live. *Operators:* the bypass flag, reserved groups, the last-holder guard; with the bypass on the operator still reads every row, which the instance operator is. *Residual:* no real step-up re-authentication until the IdP work (session age stands in); a person holding a direct group holds it everywhere by definition; records of a closed node are invisible to scoped holders; timing differences between hidden and missing records are not claimed away; direct store writes, seeds and restore are unchecked; ids are UUIDv7, so anyone who sees an `own` value learns a creation time (as with every obop id); the waiver texts are the authors' statements, checked only for length and shape.

**Acceptance tests (named).** Each name is a test its work package must add; a package is not done until its names exist and pass on memory, SQLite and PostgreSQL where storage matters. The complete lists are in the specifications; these are the ones the record rests on. *Persons and identity:* `person_id::mint_is_uuidv7_lowercase`, `person_id::adopted_refuses_reserved_prefixes_and_slashes`, `person_id::adopted_has_exactly_one_caller`, `person::delete_is_refused_for_every_group_including_admin`, `auth_login::alias_resolves_at_password_totp_second_step_and_oauth_page`, `auth_login::passkey_login_stays_keyed_by_id`, `auth_login::two_processes_cannot_claim_one_alias`, `auth_login::rename_changes_no_token_grant_or_passkey`, `create::person_account_is_one_batch_failure_leaves_nothing`, `create::generated_password_is_returned_once_and_not_logged`, `compat::legacy_users_own_rules_and_tokens_are_unchanged`, `atomic::every_account_writer_commits_change_and_event_together`. *Organisation:* `org_index::tree_matches_recursive_reference_over_1000_random_moves`, `org_index::cycle_two_parents_wrong_level_duplicate_key_are_refused`, `org_index::future_dated_move_cannot_close_a_cycle`, `org_index::corrupt_tree_is_detached_not_fatal`, `org_index::second_process_sees_a_new_epoch`, `org_index::as_of_both_sides_of_a_change_date`, `membership::per_day_oracle_for_overlap_main_and_gap`, `identifier::sixty_case_matching_fixture`, `identifier::two_writers_one_active_value_exactly_one_succeeds`, `feed::replay_adds_zero_rows_and_keeps_human_fields`, `leave::crash_after_each_step_then_rerun_equals_clean_run`, `leave::ends_every_credential_and_enable_is_refused`, `reassign::needs_platform_admin_and_moves_only_the_own_field`, `node::close_reports_stamped_records`. *Grants and epoch:* `resolution::direct_only_user_is_byte_for_byte_unchanged`, `resolution::matches_a_naive_reference_over_random_histories`, `resolution::boundary_ends_and_starts_without_a_write`, `epoch::bump_is_in_the_grant_batch`, `epoch::org_and_grant_writes_share_one_counter`, `epoch::every_listed_write_bumps_and_no_other`, `epoch::cache_ms_above_5000_refuses_to_start`, `grants::lifecycle_and_invariants`, `grants::one_open_grant_per_key_across_processes`, `grants::adopt_changes_nobody_s_effective_groups`, `grants::flip_refused_while_a_person_would_lose_a_group`, `grants::offboard_ends_grants_in_one_batch`, `approve::runs_with_live_authority_intersected`, `connector::principal_is_live_and_scoped`, `reach::unresolved_reach_denies_scope_grants`, `reach::scoped_only_holder_without_scope_rule_sees_nothing`, `log::every_kind_writes_exactly_one_event_in_its_batch`, `explain::agrees_with_outcome_for_10000_pairs`, `explain::reads_no_stored_record`. *Rules:* `legacy_gate_and_policy_agree_on_100k_random_cases`, `restrict_only_narrows`, `residual_vectors_json`, `scope_grants_node_and_descendants_not_siblings_or_parent`, `unscoped_hold_of_a_scope_group_sees_every_row`, `root_scope_equals_see_all_on_stamped_rows`, `create_stamps_and_refuses_foreign_nodes`, `owner_cannot_stamp_a_foreign_node`, `write_table_splits_read_from_write`, `list_and_search_ignore_the_write_table`, `via_write_follows_parent_write`, `related_follows_member_rows_next_request`, `member_caps_refuse_501st_and_201st`, `write_group_allows_unchanged_echo`, `action_only_field_set_only_by_effects`, `filter_on_conditional_field_is_unknown_field`, `bypass_off_denies_admin_business_rows`, `agent_writes_propose`, `approver_must_see_the_record`, `denied_is_403_on_rest`, `denied_marker_never_reaches_a_client`. *Side channels:* `s1_blob_held_by_two_records`, `s1_blob_held_only_by_hidden_is_not_found`, `s2_totals_skip_hidden_children`, `s2_all_keeps_the_old_sum`, `s3_unique_message_hidden_equals_visible`, `s4_body_byte_equal_to_a_model_without_the_field`, `s6_hidden_equals_missing_byte_equal`, `s7_hidden_children_neither_copied_nor_counted`, `s7_500_hidden_do_not_block`, `s9_discovery_names_nothing_withheld`, `s10_bulk_applies_write_and_stamp`, `s11_events_carry_no_row_data`, `reads_register_matches_source`. *Aggregate-only:* `aggregate_only_list_is_refused`, `aggregate_cohort_and_secondary_suppression`, `suppression_identical_on_rest_mcp_cli`, `min_bucket_refuses_day`, `aggregate_response_has_no_total`. *Proof:* one test per lint L1 to L19, `proof::personal_data_without_rules_refused`, `proof::waiver_visible_in_four_places`, `proof::waiver_does_not_excuse_an_ungrouped_special_category_field`, `proof::scoped_bundle_refused_until_a_scope_rule_exists`, `proof::warn_mode_installs_with_warnings`, `proof::installed_modules_never_run_the_proof_at_load`, `bundled_catalog_gate_report` (golden: 30 flagged, 5 with rules, 25 waived), `builtin_modules_pass_the_access_proof`, `admin_group_uses_are_classified`, `no_email_literals_in_security_paths`, `route_register_matches_source`. *Across topics:* `integration::scoped_grant_over_org_tree_sees_exactly_the_subtree_on_every_read_path`, `integration::future_dated_move_changes_scope_at_midnight_without_a_write`, `integration::leave_ends_grants_memberships_and_tokens_in_one_run`, `integration::closed_node_drops_out_of_every_set`, `integration::org_model_rules_follow_scoped_roles`. *The proof of the proof, in `obop-accesstest`:* the independent reference oracles (resolution, rows, organisation; a different author, each under 500 lines, never importing the engine's rule code), `oracle::engine_equals_reference_on_all_read_paths` over get, list pages, search with `total`, aggregate, ref validation, blob read, delete referrers, actions and effects, runnable actions and CSV and connector payloads (20,000 seeded pairs per pull request, 100,000 nightly, shrinking into `tests/access/regressions/`), `oracle::writes_equal_reference`, `oracle::planted_defect_found_within_1000_pairs`, `non_interference_world_pairs`, the generated cross-scope matrix `matrix::every_model_command_and_path_has_a_row_and_matches_it` (personas times models times the 20 read and write paths times REST, MCP and CLI times session, agent and OAuth credentials; expectations from the reference unless a row of `tests/access/baseline.toml` says otherwise; an unknown cell or an unexplained skip fails), `matrix::known_gaps_fail_when_the_register_is_ignored`, and `matrix::mutations_are_caught` (the de-duplicated union of the three designs' seeded weakenings, each must fail at least one cell; the feature `access-mutants` cannot be built in release). Gates: `cargo fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` with PostgreSQL, `vp check`, `vp build`, `i18n:check`, Playwright `organisation.spec.ts`, `access.spec.ts`, `people.spec.ts` (unchanged and green).

**Why.** Today obop's access control is groups plus five record rules. It cannot say who a person is (`own` holds free text that nothing validates), where a person sits in an organisation, which part of it a role applies to or for how long, who approved that, or what a person could see at a given moment; a write is allowed wherever a read is; aggregates, blobs, computed totals and effect children ignore the rules, so a person who may see one record can learn about hidden ones; `platform.admin` satisfies every check; an agent's approval runs with the rights it had when it proposed. The first downstream project built a module on obop and recorded the same defect classes (one predicate for read, write, delete and action; a computed share list that grants nobody; no write protection for a field or for fields filled by evidence; an ungated child read in effects; admin satisfying every group; agent approvals) and two architecture records for the deviations it made because obop could not express them. A business with offices and units needs these generically. Doing it as one change, with one person id, one organisation port, one epoch and one rule language, avoids three subsystems that each carry a private copy of "who is this and where do they sit".

**Rejected.** Keeping the person registry in `auth_user` (it is outside the registry, so no `ref` can point at it). Deriving the id from an e-mail address, a principal name or an IdP subject (it changes when the person's mail changes; every `own` value would have to be rewritten). Making the UUID the login name (nobody types it; a rename of an alias must not touch a token). An `org_person.invite` command that returns a temporary password (a password cannot leave the Dispatcher). Random v4 ids through an engine change (a marginal gain; every id in obop is v7). Stored mirrors of the current parent and main node (stale at a date boundary with no scheduler). A second epoch for the organisation (the grants' node sets are computed from the tree). A second port to the tree (`ScopeSource`, `ScopeTree`) beside `OrgDirectory`. `access_policy` records and rules stored as data in grants (a change to a rule is a package upgrade with a plan, audit and rollback). A profile keyed on `requires_core` (the core version stays 0.2.0 and all 22 packages say `^0.2`). Any lint inside `check_module` (`load_installed` calls it, so an installed package would vanish at the next start). An overload of `search` for aggregate-only access (one permission group per command). A per-model "exclude the operator" flag beside the global bypass switch. Per-row forward lookups for `related` (one inverse lookup per request instead). A `Viewer` or `Seen` newtype refactor of every engine file (the rename to `raw_*`, a register test and a world-pair test give almost the same guarantee). Polling the epoch every second (not "the next request"). A kv counter as the epoch (outside `apply_checked`, so a crash leaves caches stale). A `shared_with` length cap that silently revokes long lists. Pushing authority into groups only (no dates, no scope, no approval trail). Database row-level security as the primary mechanism (D10 keeps it as a later backstop).

**Consequences.** *Modules and crates added:* built-in `org` (15 stored models and `org_problem`, always on, hidden from users without `org.staff`) and `access` (2 stored and 4 virtual models: `access_group`, `access_decision`, `access_rule`, `access_batch`), the kernel crate `obop-access`, the dev-only crate `obop-accesstest`, `docs/spec/access.md`, `docs/spec/organisation.md`, labels in de, fr, pl, fi, sv, es for both modules. *Groups:* `org.staff`, `org.person_admin`, `org.node_admin`, `org.directory_contact`, `org.feed_operator`, `access.grant_delegate`, `access.grant_admin`, `access.role_admin`; `audit.reader` and `security.officer` as before. *Security event kinds:* the person kinds (`person_create`, `person_adopt`, `person_login`, `person_activate`, `person_activate_failed`, `person_suspend`, `person_reinstate`, `person_leave`, `person_rehire`), the grant and role kinds (`grant_propose`, `grant_confirm`, `grant_reject`, `grant_end`, `grant_shorten`, `grant_sweep`, `grant_import`, `access_adopt`, `role_define`, `role_groups`, `role_authority`, `role_details`, `role_retire`, `package_roles`, `install_waiver`, `explain_used`, `who_can_used`, `plan_approve`, `access_integrity`) and `aggregate_only`, `scope_resolve_error`. *Settings and flags:* `security.access_gate`, `step_up_secs`, `direct_groups`, `conflicts`, `cover_max_days`; environment `OBOP_ADMIN_BYPASS`, `OBOP_DISCLOSE_WITHHELD`, `OBOP_ACCESS_EPOCH_CACHE_MS`, `OBOP_ACCESS_MAX_ACTIVE_GRANTS` (50,000); metrics `obop_permission_epoch`, `obop_access_generation_rebuilds_total`, `obop_access_resolve_errors_total`, `obop_access_epoch_read_errors_total`. *Wire:* errors `denied` (403) and `reauth_required` (400) are rows of the one error table of step 1; `GENERATED_COMMANDS` gains `aggregate`; `CommandDef` gains `record` (`no_record()`), so a command that acts on no record has no required `id` in its MCP schema and its OpenAPI body lists its fields; `/api/meta` gains `me`, `fields_withheld`, per-model rules in kinds and names only, `waiver`, `modules[].groups`, `writable`; `GET /api/auth/users` rows gain `person` and `login`; `GET /api/auth/groups` gains a catalogue. *Amended texts:* D58 (rules now have positions; the sentence that computed totals and aggregates ignore the rules), D57's consequence sentence on aggregates, D56 (effects read visible rows), D69 (account writes are atomic with their event;
